merging all fields into one slot may turn a nearby integer field into a
handle-derived value. That would be a different verdict even though it
is conservative for aliasing.

| Alternative | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Unbounded explicit enumeration | Same reference semantics, often impractical. Not required by the existing text. |
| Normative widening with the bead's thresholds | Deterministic only with further rules for ordering/stickiness/joins; can reject previously admitted programs. Not acceptable without a capability-preserving lowering for every newly coarse case. |
| Exact symbolic sets and translation-cycle closure (recommended) | Preserves verdicts and handles this witness compactly. Does not claim every SQLite recurrence has this form. |
| Resource/time cap with an explicit unfinished build | Honest operational outcome, not a completed analysis or a source rejection. Remains implementation work. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

Add a dialect K/clarification adjacent to §5.1's K and §5.2's least
fixpoint, in both maintained maps:

```text
The reference bounds INT atoms as above; it imposes no additional
cardinality bound on pointer-offset sets or memory intervals. Implementers
MAY use exact symbolic sets, shared interval relations and SCC
acceleration instead of enumerating represented offsets or slots.
All reference sets and byte/representation-width distinctions must be
preserved for verdicts, candidate sets and dispatch domains. A coarser
summary is not the reference result merely because it is sound for
aliasing. An unfinished analysis reports an unfinished build with the
rule and exhausted implementation resource, not a source rejection,
completed verdict or run-time fault for a valid input.

In a pure modular-translation inclusion SCC, if every cycle transfer is
an unfiltered fixed displacement at one bit width W and preserves one
base/provenance coordinate, close the generated displacements as a
subgroup of Z/(2^W). For a single self-cycle of stride s, each seed a
denotes the coset a modulo gcd(s,2^W). Union distinct seed cosets. For
multiple vertices, retain vertex-specific path displacements; do not
merge their seed coordinates. Apply the same rule to a memory relation
only when every transfer preserves the complete storage interval and
representation width and all PT6–PT8 read/overlap results are identical
to the represented individual slots. Retain every ROOT and FN alternative
explicitly. Otherwise use the original rules or another exact method.
PT2's pointer-index width and preserved high pointer bits still apply;
this rule does not replace them by unrestricted full-pointer addition.
```

Add to §24:

```text
Analysis representation/acceleration is implementation policy; the
default result is the exact §5 least fixpoint, including existing K=4096
INT abstraction. Time or memory caps do not alter that result. A report
of unfinished analysis keeps the remaining implementation obligation open.
```

This recommends an exact acceleration and explicitly **declines new
verdict-affecting bounds**. If council instead wants a new abstract
domain, it must supply canonical joins, evaluation order independence,
ROOT/partial-access rules and a lowering that preserves all programs
previously in scope; the current bead does not establish those properties.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

An exact representation clarification changes no materialised program,
ISA object/certificate or catalog bytes. A dialect publication still
gets its required revision/mapping identity. A threshold widening could
change descriptors, dispatch domains, diagnostics and emitted object
hashes, so cannot masquerade as a local plugin optimization. Plugin and
independent dialect checker should compare normalized exact results on
small-width cases, not implementation data-structure counts.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Enumerate all W=8, stride-8 cases above and compare their symbolic cosets;
W=64 is a symbolic cardinality test, never a 2^61-element allocation.
Test stride 0, stride 1, multiple seeds, a cycle with a filter (must not
use the pure-cycle rule), and GEP with I(a)<P(a). Memory vectors include
same-width full copies, overlapping partial copies and adjacent handle/
integer fields; retain PT6's non-handle top and ROOT distinctions. Check
identical verdicts independent of worklist order. Mathematical cosets are
checked by [contract-verification](2026-11-02-contract-verification.md).
The SQLite exact fixpoint and production gate remain unverified here.

---
name: beads
description: Use when working in a repository that uses bd or Beads for durable project task tracking, issue dependencies, blocker management, multi-session handoff, or shared work memory. Trigger when the user asks to find ready work, claim or close tasks, create follow-up work, inspect blockers, recover project context, or choose between local planning and persistent project tracking.
---

# Beads

Use Beads as the shared project task system. Local plans, scratch files, and personal memories are useful, but they are not the durable source of truth for project work.

## First Step

Run:

```bash
bd prime
```

If that prints nothing, check whether the repository has an active Beads workspace:

```bash
bd where
```

## Preferred Route

Use the `bd` CLI when shell access is available. It is the most compact and direct Beads interface.

## Core CLI Workflow

1. Find work:

```bash
bd ready
bd list --status=open
bd list --status=in_progress
```

2. Inspect before editing:

```bash
bd show <id>
```

3. Claim work atomically:

```bash
bd update <id> --claim
```

4. Create durable follow-up work when implementation reveals new tasks:

```bash
bd create "Short title" --description="Why this exists and what needs to be done" --type=task --priority=2
```

5. Close completed work:

```bash
bd close <id> --reason="Completed"
```

## What Belongs In Beads

Use Beads for:

- shared project tasks
- blockers and dependencies
- discovered follow-up work
- work that must survive thread reset, compaction, or handoff
- status that another person or agent should be able to resume

Use agent-local planning tools only for the current turn's execution checklist. Do not treat them as shared project state.

## Rules

- Do not create markdown TODO files as the source of truth when Beads is available.
- Do not use `bd edit`; it opens an interactive editor. Use `bd update` flags instead.
- Prefer `--json` when parsing `bd` output programmatically.
- If hooks are installed, `bd prime` may already be injected. Run it manually when context is missing.
- Do not auto-close or mutate tasks unless the work is actually complete.

# 5u3 — exact pointer fixpoints without verdict-changing caps

Proposal for the dialect council; `b-dialect-spec-5u3`, linked to
`bennett-plugin` bp-fj1. This job writes no dialect or plugin source.

## Defect classification and North Star scope

Both dialect `Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md` (draft 0.2.12) and the 0.3 draft
§5 require every verdict/candidate/dispatch domain to come from the
specified reference analysis. §5.1 caps INT atoms at K=4096, but does not
cap explicit object offsets or PT6–PT8 memory intervals. PT2/PT5 use
finite-width modular pointer arithmetic. Hence an explicit enumeration
is finite but can have 2^61 members. §5.1 already admits residue classes
and `*`; it does not require enumerative storage.

The bead establishes a severe implementation/performance problem, **not
by itself an inconsistent or undefined reference semantics**. A sound
over-approximation may still change a compile-time verdict, which §5
forbids. The proposed thresholds (4096 offsets, 4 outside extent, 32 for
unknown extent; max(64,extent/4) slots) are not justified as exact. They
must not become normative merely because one plugin uses them.

North Star §§0/3.3/6.1 require arbitrary defined bounded programs,
whole-program analysis and substitutable compilers; §8 rung 4 requires
unmodified SQLite. Widening that introduces a false handle-use/escape
rejection narrows those capabilities. Removing SQLite features, changing
its source, suppressing vectorisation or declaring valid pointer patterns
unsupported does not resolve the issue. Keep bp-fj1 open for practical
convergence; do not declare the rung delivered from a coarse fixpoint.

## Plugin evidence actually inspected

`../bennett-plugin-rung4/.tmp/rung4/PROGRESS.md` reports 177381 slots
plateauing by round 7 on the N=16, LLVM-18 closure, with additional atoms
still growing. The tail of `.tmp/rung4/prof/trace-k.log` shows round 39,
177381 slots, growth in SQLite integer/value sets, then
`§5.2 PT8/PT9/PT12 budget exhausted` at a memcpy in
`@sqlite3CreateForeignKey`, exit 1 after 3401.31 seconds. It does **not**
demonstrate a converged exact reference fixpoint or prove that remaining
growth is only a pointer chain. The supplied earlier 800k-slot report is
historical bead evidence, not reproduced here. No full sibling gate was
run, and no plugin implementation was copied.

## Alternatives and worked recurrence

Take width W=64 and an unfiltered inclusion cycle with one provenance X:

```text
S initially contains offset 0
S includes { (o+8) mod 2^64 : o in S }
```

Its exact least solution is `{o: o=0 mod 8, 0<=o<2^64}`. There are
`2^64/gcd(8,2^64)=2^61` offsets, representable by one residue class.
For W=8 the set is exactly 0,8,...,248 (32 offsets), followed by 0.
Starting at 3 instead gives 3,11,...,251; adding a seed at 4 gives two
cosets, not `*`. Extent=16 does not permit dropping offsets ≥16:
PT2 retains original provenance and adds EXT/top for possibly invalid
addresses; it does not turn numerical out-of-range into host escape.

A full-width same-object memory copy from offset o to o+8 can similarly
propagate one stored atom along that relation. An exact periodic memory
summary must retain storage width, intervals and value/root provenance;
merging all fields into one slot may turn a nearby integer field into a
handle-derived value. That would be a different verdict even though it
is conservative for aliasing.

| Alternative | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Unbounded explicit enumeration | Same reference semantics, often impractical. Not required by the existing text. |
| Normative widening with the bead's thresholds | Deterministic only with further rules for ordering/stickiness/joins; can reject previously admitted programs. Not acceptable without a capability-preserving lowering for every newly coarse case. |
| Exact symbolic sets and translation-cycle closure (recommended) | Preserves verdicts and handles this witness compactly. Does not claim every SQLite recurrence has this form. |
| Resource/time cap with an explicit unfinished build | Honest operational outcome, not a completed analysis or a source rejection. Remains implementation work. |

1020:| PT1 | `alloca`, global `@G`, allocation-site call | AV ∋ (X, 0); zero-filled initial extents are seeded as above |
1021:| PT2 | `getelementptr T, ptr p, idx…` | uses P(a), I(a) and the complete finite-width computation of §16.4, also for constant expressions: sequential indices are sign-extended/truncated to I(a), scaling and offset accumulation wrap modulo 2^I(a), and addition preserves pointer bits above I(a). Zero accumulated offset copies AV(p). For every (X,o), include the image of all represented base addresses and index values (unknown base bits range over patterns consistent with DataLayout and X's alignment), using integer/residue-class/`*` offset unions. Unbounded mathematical addition is not a substitute. If an address may leave X, include (EXT, `*`) and the non-handle top flag; PT6 propagates this unknown-address alternative. The unknown-address alternative does not by itself establish host escape (§6.4): retain the original base provenance for that test; actual host/unknown origins keep their restrictions. ROOT provenance and §6.2 restrictions remain explicit. Array dimensions determine stride, never an index bound |
1022:| PT3 | `phi`, `select` (value operands), `freeze`, `addrspacecast`; `bitcast` | the first four copy (union). A bitcast copies lane by lane only when source and destination have identical lane counts and per-lane representation widths, treating a scalar as one lane. Otherwise use the virtual N21 bitcast expansion of §12.6: pack source-lane bit patterns into its prescribed bit string in DataLayout order, then resegment at the destination representation width. Compute output AV through PT4/PT5 transfers of that expansion; finite INT lane sets denote their full Cartesian product, and K applies per resulting lane. Never copy a source-lane INT atom as the value of a packed destination scalar. Unknown bits propagate non-handle top through those transfers. Explicit object and ROOT provenance follows the same expansion and remains subject to §6.2; top never substitutes for ROOT. Apply also to constant expressions and fixed-vscale forms during S2 before verdicts, candidate sets, shapes and dispatch domains freeze. Direct finite bit-string evaluation is equivalent; no LLVM optimisation is required |
1023:| PT4 | `ptrtoint`, `inttoptr`, `zext`/`sext`/`trunc` | copy only bit-preserving conversions. Otherwise apply the actual source/destination-width bit-vector conversion (§16.4): ptrtoint/inttoptr zero-extend or truncate at P(a), sext sign-extends. Use PT2’s represented-address image construction, retaining originating object and ROOT provenance. A narrowed representation that cannot be reassociated with its original object adds (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤; numerical uncertainty is not host escape. Apply also to constant expressions |
1024:| PT5 | `add`/`sub` of a value with pointer atoms and an `INT` set | computes address bits modulo 2^W at the integer operation’s width W, never unbounded offset addition. Relative displacements from the originating base are modular too: zero net displacement represents that base again, including after an inttoptr truncation to P(a). Use PT2’s represented-address image construction at W bits (full-width arithmetic, no preserved high GEP bits), retaining original object and ROOT provenance. Keep modular displacement alternatives even outside the allocation, and additionally include (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤ when an image may leave it. Residue classes and `*` take the same finite-width image; an unrepresentable image retains (X, `*`) for each originating X plus (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤. Other integer operations on pointer atoms use that conservative image. Widening a pointer-derived integer by zext/sext uses that conservative image too (fixed reference fallback); ptrtoint zero-extension retains its base representation. Numerical uncertainty does not itself establish host escape (PT2). The rules apply also to constant expressions, before candidate/dispatch domains freeze; on finite `INT` sets, every LLVM integer operation, cast and `icmp` is evaluated elementwise; with x ⊤ and a constant m, `and x, m` gives the submasks of m and `urem x, m` gives {0, …, m−1} (each still subject to K); a range attribute or `!range` metadata removed by N3/N4 is not a bound on materialised execution and MUST NOT restrict any verdict, candidate set, descriptor shape or dispatch domain. Only bounds independently established by materialised instructions restrict the set; otherwise retain ⊤ and apply the ordinary operation rules, including finite masks/remainders |
1025:| PT6 | `load T, ptr p` | for each (X,o) ∈ AV(p): memory atoms carry their storage interval and representation width; copy a whole-value atom only for that identical interval with a compatible representation. A known immutable byte image decodes exactly the accessed bytes using DataLayout and T (including initialisers). For a partial, spanning or overlapping access whose result bits are not represented exactly, the non-handle value is ⊤, never the union of component INT values. X=EXT gives ⊤. A non-handle top flag on p contributes the same unknown-address load alternative even when explicit object offsets overlap no known slot; it MUST NOT be dropped. ROOT provenance remains explicit and §6.2's partial-handle restrictions still apply. For a possibly valid load wholly contained in a zero-filled initial extent, include the all-zero value of the load’s representation and width, unioned with values from every overlapping store or copy under these interval rules. PT8 propagates these initial byte possibilities when copying memory. Zero contributes no ROOT. Region inputs, replayed caller bytes and other nonzero-initialised contents retain their specified sources; this rule does not replace them with zeros |
1026:| PT7 | `store v, ptr p` | retain each written interval and representation width; partial writes overlapping a larger load participate in PT6's conservative fallback. AV(X, o + d_k) ⊇ AV(v_k) for each (X, o) ∈ AV(p) and each lane or field k at byte offset d_k |
1027:| PT8 | memory copy (`llvm.memcpy`/`memmove`/`memcpy.inline`, `__bennett_rt_memmove`) of (dst, src, len); memory fill (`llvm.memset`, `__bennett_rt_memset`) of (dst, v, len) | copy: propagate zero-byte initial images over the copied byte ranges, including their symbolic extents; for each slot (Y, s) of each (Y, o_s) ∈ AV(src) and (Z, o_d) ∈ AV(dst): when offsets and len are constants and the atom's entire storage interval [s, s + size(atom)) lies in the copied range, copy its atom and representation width to (Z, o_d + s − o_s). Every partially overlapping atom instead contributes non-handle ⊤ and explicit ROOT provenance to the affected destination interval (unknown offsets/len use `*`); do not copy the whole component INT. Fill: every slot in [o_d, o_d + len) of each (Z, o_d) ∈ AV(dst) (all of Z when an offset or len is not constant) gets the `INT` atoms of the byte v repeated to the slot's width (⊤ when v is not constant) |
1028:| PT9 | direct call of G, or indirect call with candidate G (§14.2) | AV(parameter i of G) ⊇ AV(argument i); AV(result) ⊇ the union of AV over G's `ret` operands; `byval` arguments copy the pointee's slots into the callee's copy object |
1029:| PT10 | `insertelement`/`extractelement`/`shufflevector`/`insertvalue`/`extractvalue` | extraction/insertion use N15’s total unsigned index semantics. Constant in-range extraction copies its lane; constant out-of-range extraction gives the element type’s zero. Dynamic extraction unions every source lane selected by a represented index, plus zero iff the index set includes an unsigned value >= L (including top when 2^K > L). No zero is added if every represented index is in range; zero adds no ROOT. In-range insertion replaces the selected lane; out-of-range insertion preserves the vector. Dynamic insertion includes each old lane unless it is certainly replaced, and the inserted value whenever that lane can be selected. 6795:### 13.1 Soft-float rewrite (rules N19, N32)
6823:| `llvm.sqrt`, `llvm.floor`, `llvm.ceil`, `llvm.trunc`, `llvm.round`, `llvm.roundeven`, `llvm.rint`, `llvm.nearbyint`, `llvm.canonicalize` | `C @__bennett_sf_<name>_B(C)` | bitcast |
6834:For each such name fmt and each B of §13.1, N32 MUST emit `iN @__bennett_sf_convert_to_<fmt>_B(C_B, i32 R, i1 Sat)` for to-arbitrary (format MDString removed, rounding encoded by §13.5, immediate saturation retained), and `C_B @__bennett_sf_convert_from_<fmt>_B(iN)` for from-arbitrary; fmt is spelled exactly as its case-sensitive MDString, N is its listed bit width, and results are integer bits / bitcast B respectively. Finite conversions use the named format's rounding, saturation and nonfinite rules with canonical NaNs; N1 defines a source-poison conversion result as zero bits, including unsaturated out-of-range FP4/FP6 inputs. A missing APFloat lowering is an implementation obligation, never permission to reject a valid named format or emit an unresolved numerical placeholder.
6836:**Static instantiation request v1.** The driver MUST collect required concrete arith instances before the final link and supply a UTF-8 JSON object with exactly `schema: "bennett-arith-instantiations-1"`, `llvm_major` (integer), `target_triple` (string), `data_layout` (LLVM string), and `entries` (array); a conversion entry has exactly `op: "convert_to" | "convert_from"`, `format` (the MDString above), and `base` (B suffix), and a powi entry has exactly `op: "powi"`, `base`, and `exponent_bits` (integer K, 1..8388608). A powi entry requests both `C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<B>_i<K>(C_B, iK)` and `C_B @__bennett_sf_powi_<B>_i<K>_rm(C_B, iK, i32 R)` (the suffix `_rm` is last); a conversion entry requests its exact signature above. Duplicate entries denote one instance; canonical request bytes are JCS with entries sorted by (op, base, format or empty string, exponent_bits or zero).
6849:intervenes. For canonical temporary slots, emit ss_push(8L,8), then limb
6863:observable flags or exceptions (except §13.5); **every NaN result is the format's canonical NaN**
6866:the f64 canonical NaN in the high double and +0 in the low); `rem` is C `fmod` (exact, the sign of
6872:operand; two: the canonical NaN) with −0 < +0; `minimum`/`maximum` are IEEE 754-2019 (either NaN:
6873:the canonical NaN) with −0 < +0; `minimumnum`/`maximumnum` are IEEE 754-2019 minimumNumber/
6876:nearest even; `canonicalize` maps every NaN to the canonical NaN and is the identity otherwise;
6887:For these guarantees, a finite canonical double-double is a nonoverlapping pair (h,l) of binary64 values with h = roundTiesToEven(h+l) and l the exact residual (positive zero when the residual is zero), zero represented as (signed zero,+0); the result MUST be the canonical pair whose exact sum is nearest the exact mathematical result (or its directed neighbour for §13.5), with ties to the even low-component significand, using the high significand when the low component is zero, and NaN/inf represented in the high component with low +0. Overflow is determined by rounding with an unbounded high exponent first and then applying binary64's high-component exponent limit with IEEE overflow outcomes for the active mode (the finite maximum or infinity as appropriate); underflow uses tininess after rounding of the exact pair sum, with subnormal threshold 2^-1022.
6891:Constrained operations covered by these guarantees MUST derive invalid/divbyzero/overflow/underflow/inexact from the mathematical operation and final rounding, not the status of an inaccurate APFloat fallback or an intermediate high/low operation; for sqrt/fma/conversion, inexact means the final pair's exact sum differs from the exact finite result, and underflow requires both tininess after rounding and inexactness. Rounding-to-integral follows the named IEEE operation: rint/lrint/llrint signal inexact on a nonintegral finite operand, nearbyint suppresses inexact, and floor/ceil/trunc/round/roundeven/lround/llround do not raise inexact merely for discarding a fractional part. Other PPC operations retain their named major-specific APFloat semantics subject to the explicit canonical-NaN rules above.

6949:**Accuracy envelope.** For each finite mathematical result of a §13.4 own-format carrier transcendental, the implementation MUST return a B value within one local ulp of that exact result, over the entire finite input domain; local ulp is the spacing of B's adjacent finite values bracketing the exact result, taking an exactly representable result as the lower endpoint (the upper endpoint at the largest finite value), with the least positive subnormal spacing at zero or in the subnormal interval. For ppcf128 use §13.1's ordered canonical-pair values. Special values, domain errors, poles, signed zeros and overflow results follow the named operation's mathematical/IEEE semantics rather than this finite-result envelope. At overflow the result is the correctly rounded overflow outcome for the requested mode. This is an error bound, not a general correct-rounding claim.

# Contract derivations — review index and reproducible verification

These are proposals for council grading, not published spec changes.
Contract beads remain open for acceptance/implementation; b5b's owner
handles its implementation status. No git command was run by this job.

A recommendation for a 0.9 K repair means the existing council-reviewed
repair track, not permission to publish an incompatible change silently.
`spec/README.md` normally requires a full-version bump for semantic
changes. Council must settle that publication choice. Every statement
below that existing source hashes stay fixed assumes an authorized
in-version repair with unchanged envelope bytes; a full-version bump
instead changes all symbol/interface/closure envelopes and their consumer
pins. Catalog bytes/hash change only when that separately versioned
catalog is actually changed. No note here performs either publication.

## Exact arithmetic and document check

Run this extraction from the repository root under the environment prefix:

```sh
python3 -I -B - <<'PY'
from pathlib import Path
p = Path('docs/notes/2026-11-02-contract-verification.md')
source = p.read_text().split('```python\n', 1)[1].split('\n```', 1)[0]
exec(compile(source, str(p), 'exec'))
PY
```

The arithmetic is in Q[z]/(z^4+1), z=exp(i*pi/4), with rational
coefficients; H uses (z−z³)/2. There are no floating-point quantum
calculations in this check. Equality of every branch operator to V/sqrt(2)
establishes the resulting channel on all matrix units, hence also with
an external reference; the negative vectors compare density matrices.
This is exact finite mathematical verification, not a producer certificate
accepted by an implementation of the proposed contract.

```python
from fractions import Fraction as F
from pathlib import Path
from math import gcd
import hashlib
import re

def scalar(n):
    return (F(n), F(0), F(0), F(0))

ZERO, ONE = scalar(0), scalar(1)
Z = (F(0), F(1), F(0), F(0))

def add(a, b):
    return tuple(x + y for x, y in zip(a, b))

def neg(a):
    return tuple(-x for x in a)

def mul(a, b):
    out = [F(0)] * 7
    for i in range(4):
        for j in range(4):
            out[i+j] += a[i] * b[j]
    for k in range(6, 3, -1):
        out[k-4] -= out[k]
    return tuple(out[:4])

def conj(a):
    out = [a[0], F(0), F(0), F(0)]
    for k in range(1, 4):
        out[4-k] -= a[k]
    return tuple(out)

I = mul(Z, Z)
TINV = conj(Z)
HFACTOR = (F(0), F(1, 2), F(0), F(-1, 2))
assert mul(HFACTOR, HFACTOR) == scalar(F(1, 2))
assert mul(Z, TINV) == ONE
assert mul(I, I) == neg(ONE)

def compact(state):
    return {i: a for i, a in state.items() if a != ZERO}

def accumulate(state, i, a):
    state[i] = add(state.get(i, ZERO), a)

def enabled(i, controls):
    return all((i >> c) & 1 for c in controls)

def hadamard(state, wire, controls=()):
    out = {}
    mask = 1 << wire
    for i, a in state.items():
        if not enabled(i, controls):
            accumulate(out, i, a)
            continue
        a = mul(a, HFACTOR)
        low = i & ~mask
        accumulate(out, low, a)
        accumulate(out, low | mask, neg(a) if i & mask else a)
    return compact(out)

def flip(state, target, controls=()):
    out = {}
    for i, a in state.items():
        j = i ^ (1 << target) if enabled(i, controls) else i
        accumulate(out, j, a)
    return compact(out)

def phase(state, target, factor, controls=()):
    return compact({i: mul(a, factor) if (i >> target) & 1
                    and enabled(i, controls) else a
                    for i, a in state.items()})

# Bit positions: a=0, b=1, g=2, y=3, outer q=4.
C = [('h', 2), ('t', 2), ('cx', (0, 2)), ('tdg', 2),
     ('cx', (1, 2)), ('t', 2), ('cx', (0, 2)),
     ('tdg', 2), ('h', 2), ('sdg', 2)]
adj = {'h': 'h', 'cx': 'cx', 't': 'tdg', 'tdg': 't',
       's': 'sdg', 'sdg': 's'}
CDAG = [(adj[op], args) for op, args in reversed(C)]

def sequence(state, gates, controls=()):
    for op, args in gates:
        if op == 'h':
            state = hadamard(state, args, controls)
        elif op == 'cx':
            state = flip(state, args[1], (args[0],) + controls)
        else:
            factor = {'t': Z, 'tdg': TINV, 's': I, 'sdg': neg(I)}[op]
            state = phase(state, args, factor, controls)
    return state

for a in range(2):
    for b in range(2):
        i = a | (b << 1)
        assert sequence({i: ONE}, C) == {i | ((a*b) << 2): ONE}
        dirty_phase = I if (a, b) == (0, 1) else neg(I)
        assert sequence({i | 4: ONE}, C) == {
            i | ((1 ^ (a*b)) << 2): dirty_phase}

def expected(state, controlled, closed):
    outer = (4,) if controlled else ()
    state = flip(state, 3, (0, 1) + outer)
    return hadamard(state, 0, outer) if closed else state

def branches(state, controlled=False, closed=False, correction='correct'):
    outer = (4,) if controlled else ()
    state = sequence(state, C, outer)
    state = flip(state, 3, (2,) + outer)
    if closed:
        state = hadamard(state, 0, outer)
    state = hadamard(state, 2)  # Unconditional X-basis measurement.
    result = []
    for m in range(2):
        branch = {i: a for i, a in state.items() if ((i >> 2) & 1) == m}
        if m:
            branch = flip(branch, 2)  # Reset measured private wire.
            transport = closed and correction != 'stale'
            if transport:
                branch = hadamard(branch, 0, outer)
            if correction != 'none':
                controls = (0, 1) + (() if correction == 'uncontrolled' else outer)
                branch = {i: neg(a) if enabled(i, controls) else a
                          for i, a in branch.items()}
            if transport:
                branch = hadamard(branch, 0, outer)
        result.append(compact(branch))
    return result

branch_columns = 0
for controlled in (False, True):
    outer = (4,) if controlled else ()
    for i in range(32):
        if i & 4:
            continue
        start = {i: ONE}
        unitary = sequence(start, C, outer)
        unitary = flip(unitary, 3, (2,) + outer)
        unitary = sequence(unitary, CDAG, outer)
        assert unitary == expected(start, controlled, False)
        for closed in (False, True):
            want = {j: mul(a, HFACTOR)
                    for j, a in expected(start, controlled, closed).items()}
            for branch in branches(start, controlled, closed):
                assert branch == compact(want)
                branch_columns += 1
assert branch_columns == 128

def density(states):
    out = {}
    for state in states:
        for i, a in state.items():
            for j, b in state.items():
                accumulate(out, (i, j), mul(a, conj(b)))
    return compact(out)

superposition = {0: ONE}
for wire in (0, 1, 4):
    superposition = hadamard(superposition, wire)
for controlled, closed, correction in [
        (False, False, 'none'), (True, False, 'uncontrolled'),
        (False, True, 'stale')]:
    actual = density(branches(superposition, controlled, closed, correction))
    ideal = density([expected(superposition, controlled, closed)])
    assert actual != ideal, correction

# Imported model, unit conversion, sequence/parallel accounting.
def model(d):
    return 3*2*d, 2*d*d, F(1, 10) * F(1, 100)**((d+1)//2)
assert model(5) == (30, 50, F(1, 10**7))
assert model(3) == (18, 18, F(1, 100000))
assert F(30, 10**9) == F(3, 10**8)
assert 3*30*50 == 4500
assert (3*30, 50) == (90, 50)  # Sequential time/peak.
assert (30, 3*50) == (30, 150)  # Parallel time/peak.

# Width dispatch, persistent versus reversed load state.
assert (1 ^ 0, 5 ^ 3) == (1, 6)
def later_run(reverse):
    memory, bump = 7, 17  # A stored 7 and allocated at 16.
    if reverse:
        memory, bump = 0, 16
    return memory, bump
assert later_run(False) == (7, 17)
assert later_run(True) == (0, 16)

# Fixed-image coverage; hashing raw bytes differs from hashing hex text.
image_hash = hashlib.sha256(bytes([1, 2])).hexdigest()
assert image_hash != hashlib.sha256(b'0102').hexdigest()
assert 2**(1+1+4) == 64
assert F(64, 2**22) == F(1, 65536)
assert (2**16)*8 == 524288

# Exact modular orbit; no enormous enumeration.
for seed in (0, 3, 4):
    visited, current = set(), seed
    while current not in visited:
        visited.add(current)
        current = (current + 8) % 256
    assert visited == {x for x in range(256) if (x-seed) % 8 == 0}
    assert len(visited) == 32
assert (2**64)//gcd(8, 2**64) == 2**61

# Controlled X and -X produce orthogonal Bell states.
bell_plus = {0: HFACTOR, 3: HFACTOR}
bell_minus = {0: HFACTOR, 3: neg(HFACTOR)}
inner = ZERO
for i, a in bell_plus.items():
    inner = add(inner, mul(conj(a), bell_minus[i]))
assert inner == ZERO

# Fault counts concern code(F_B), not the incoming XOR word.
assert 4*4*16 == 256
assert 1 ^ 1 == 0  # Code 1 failure can leave final fault word zero.
assert 1 ^ 0 == 1  # Successful invocation can leave it nonzero.
assert (1+1, 2*2, max(1, 2)) == (2, 4, 2)  # Root time/occupancy/peak.
assert 1+2 != 2  # Child peaks cannot be summed to get root peak.

## Verification limits

The exact checks establish the finite witnesses and internal document
consistency. They do not prove the proposed general contract sound in
all cases, accept a new K entry, close an implementation bead, or show
that SQLite's full reference analysis converges in practical time.
Each item states its own remaining acceptance vectors and consumer work.

1025:| PT2 | `getelementptr T, ptr p, idx…` | uses P(a), I(a) and the complete finite-width computation of §16.4, also for constant expressions: sequential indices are sign-extended/truncated to I(a), scaling and offset accumulation wrap modulo 2^I(a), and addition preserves pointer bits above I(a). Zero accumulated offset copies AV(p). For every (X,o), include the image of all represented base addresses and index values (unknown base bits range over patterns consistent with DataLayout and X's alignment), using integer/residue-class/`*` offset unions. Unbounded mathematical addition is not a substitute. If an address may leave X, include (EXT, `*`) and the non-handle top flag; PT6 propagates this unknown-address alternative. The unknown-address alternative does not by itself establish host escape (§6.4): retain the original base provenance for that test; actual host/unknown origins keep their restrictions. ROOT provenance and §6.2 restrictions remain explicit. Array dimensions determine stride, never an index bound |
1026:| PT3 | `phi`, `select` (value operands), `freeze`, `addrspacecast`; `bitcast` | the first four copy (union). A bitcast copies lane by lane only when source and destination have identical lane counts and per-lane representation widths, treating a scalar as one lane. Otherwise use the virtual N21 bitcast expansion of §12.6: pack source-lane bit patterns into its prescribed bit string in DataLayout order, then resegment at the destination representation width. Compute output AV through PT4/PT5 transfers of that expansion; finite INT lane sets denote their full Cartesian product, and K applies per resulting lane. Never copy a source-lane INT atom as the value of a packed destination scalar. Unknown bits propagate non-handle top through those transfers. Explicit object and ROOT provenance follows the same expansion and remains subject to §6.2; top never substitutes for ROOT. Apply also to constant expressions and fixed-vscale forms during S2 before verdicts, candidate sets, shapes and dispatch domains freeze. Direct finite bit-string evaluation is equivalent; no LLVM optimisation is required |
1027:| PT4 | `ptrtoint`, `inttoptr`, `zext`/`sext`/`trunc` | copy only bit-preserving conversions. Otherwise apply the actual source/destination-width bit-vector conversion (§16.4): ptrtoint/inttoptr zero-extend or truncate at P(a), sext sign-extends. Use PT2’s represented-address image construction, retaining originating object and ROOT provenance. A narrowed representation that cannot be reassociated with its original object adds (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤; numerical uncertainty is not host escape. Apply also to constant expressions |
1028:| PT5 | `add`/`sub` of a value with pointer atoms and an `INT` set | computes address bits modulo 2^W at the integer operation’s width W, never unbounded offset addition. Relative displacements from the originating base are modular too: zero net displacement represents that base again, including after an inttoptr truncation to P(a). Use PT2’s represented-address image construction at W bits (full-width arithmetic, no preserved high GEP bits), retaining original object and ROOT provenance. Keep modular displacement alternatives even outside the allocation, and additionally include (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤ when an image may leave it. Residue classes and `*` take the same finite-width image; an unrepresentable image retains (X, `*`) for each originating X plus (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤. Other integer operations on pointer atoms use that conservative image. Widening a pointer-derived integer by zext/sext uses that conservative image too (fixed reference fallback); ptrtoint zero-extension retains its base representation. Numerical uncertainty does not itself establish host escape (PT2). The rules apply also to constant expressions, before candidate/dispatch domains freeze; on finite `INT` sets, every LLVM integer operation, cast and `icmp` is evaluated elementwise; with x ⊤ and a constant m, `and x, m` gives the submasks of m and `urem x, m` gives {0, …, m−1} (each still subject to K); a range attribute or `!range` metadata removed by N3/N4 is not a bound on materialised execution and MUST NOT restrict any verdict, candidate set, descriptor shape or dispatch domain. Only bounds independently established by materialised instructions restrict the set; otherwise retain ⊤ and apply the ordinary operation rules, including finite masks/remainders |
1029:| PT6 | `load T, ptr p` | for each (X,o) ∈ AV(p): memory atoms carry their storage interval and representation width; copy a whole-value atom only for that identical interval with a compatible representation. A known immutable byte image decodes exactly the accessed bytes using DataLayout and T (including initialisers). For a partial, spanning or overlapping access whose result bits are not represented exactly, the non-handle value is ⊤, never the union of component INT values. X=EXT gives ⊤. A non-handle top flag on p contributes the same unknown-address load alternative even when explicit object offsets overlap no known slot; it MUST NOT be dropped. ROOT provenance remains explicit and §6.2's partial-handle restrictions still apply. For a possibly valid load wholly contained in a zero-filled initial extent, include the all-zero value of the load’s representation and width, unioned with values from every overlapping store or copy under these interval rules. PT8 propagates these initial byte possibilities when copying memory. Zero contributes no ROOT. Region inputs, replayed caller bytes and other nonzero-initialised contents retain their specified sources; this rule does not replace them with zeros |
1030:| PT7 | `store v, ptr p` | retain each written interval and representation width; partial writes overlapping a larger load participate in PT6's conservative fallback. AV(X, o + d_k) ⊇ AV(v_k) for each (X, o) ∈ AV(p) and each lane or field k at byte offset d_k |
1031:| PT8 | memory copy (`llvm.memcpy`/`memmove`/`memcpy.inline`, `__bennett_rt_memmove`) of (dst, src, len); memory fill (`llvm.memset`, `__bennett_rt_memset`) of (dst, v, len) | copy: propagate zero-byte initial images over the copied byte ranges, including their symbolic extents; for each slot (Y, s) of each (Y, o_s) ∈ AV(src) and (Z, o_d) ∈ AV(dst): when offsets and len are constants and the atom's entire storage interval [s, s + size(atom)) lies in the copied range, copy its atom and representation width to (Z, o_d + s − o_s). Every partially overlapping atom instead contributes non-handle ⊤ and explicit ROOT provenance to the affected destination interval (unknown offsets/len use `*`); do not copy the whole component INT. Fill: every slot in [o_d, o_d + len) of each (Z, o_d) ∈ AV(dst) (all of Z when an offset or len is not constant) gets the `INT` atoms of the byte v repeated to the slot's width (⊤ when v is not constant) |

6844:**Static instantiation request v1.** The driver MUST collect required concrete arith instances before the final link and supply a UTF-8 JSON object with exactly `schema: "bennett-arith-instantiations-1"`, `llvm_major` (integer), `target_triple` (string), `data_layout` (LLVM string), and `entries` (array); a conversion entry has exactly `op: "convert_to" | "convert_from"`, `format` (the MDString above), and `base` (B suffix), and a powi entry has exactly `op: "powi"`, `base`, and `exponent_bits` (integer K, 1..8388608). 6895:For these guarantees, a finite canonical double-double is a nonoverlapping pair (h,l) of binary64 values with h = roundTiesToEven(h+l) and l the exact residual (positive zero when the residual is zero), zero represented as (signed zero,+0); the result MUST be the canonical pair whose exact sum is nearest the exact mathematical result (or its directed neighbour for §13.5), with ties to the even low-component significand, using the high significand when the low component is zero, and NaN/inf represented in the high component with low +0. Overflow is determined by rounding with an unbounded high exponent first and then applying binary64's high-component exponent limit with IEEE overflow outcomes for the active mode (the finite maximum or infinity as appropriate); underflow uses tininess after rounding of the exact pair sum, with subnormal threshold 2^-1022.
6899:Constrained operations covered by these guarantees MUST derive invalid/divbyzero/overflow/underflow/inexact from the mathematical operation and final rounding, not the status of an inaccurate APFloat fallback or an intermediate high/low operation; for sqrt/fma/conversion, inexact means the final pair's exact sum differs from the exact finite result, and underflow requires both tininess after rounding and inexactness. Rounding-to-integral follows the named IEEE operation: rint/lrint/llrint signal inexact on a nonintegral finite operand, nearbyint suppresses inexact, and floor/ceil/trunc/round/roundeven/lround/llround do not raise inexact merely for discarding a fractional part. Other PPC operations retain their named major-specific APFloat semantics subject to the explicit canonical-NaN rules above.

# 🚨 SESSION CLOSE PROTOCOL 🚨

**CRITICAL**: Before saying "done" or "complete", you MUST run this checklist:

```
[ ] 1. bd close <id1> <id2> ...   (close completed issues)
[ ] 2. run quality gates        (tests, linters, builds when relevant)
[ ] 3. git status               (check what changed)
[ ] 4. follow active profile    (conservative: report handoff; team-maintainer: commit/sync/push if enabled)
```

**Policy:** Conservative is the default. Commit, sync, or push only when the active user, orchestrator, or repository profile grants that authority.

## Core Rules
- **Default**: Use beads for ALL task tracking (`bd create`, `bd ready`, `bd close`)
- **Prohibited**: Do NOT use TodoWrite, TaskCreate, or markdown files for task tracking
- **Workflow**: Create beads issue BEFORE writing code, mark in_progress when starting
- **Memory**: Use `bd remember "insight"` for persistent knowledge across sessions. Do NOT use MEMORY.md files — they fragment across accounts. Search with `bd memories <keyword>`.
- Persistence you don't need beats lost context
- Default: do not commit, push, or run dolt remote sync without explicit authority. Team-maintainer behavior is opt-in and still subordinate to user/orchestrator instructions.
- Git workflow: conservative by default; commit/push only with explicit user/orchestrator or team-maintainer authority
- Session management: check `bd ready` for available work

## Essential Commands

### Finding Work
- `bd ready` - Show issues ready to work (no blockers)
- `bd list --status=open` - All open issues
- `bd list --status=in_progress` - Your active work
- `bd show <id>` - Detailed issue view with dependencies

### Creating & Updating
- `bd create --title="Summary of this issue" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task|bug|feature --priority=2` - New issue
  - Priority: 0-4 or P0-P4 (0=critical, 2=medium, 4=backlog). NOT "high"/"medium"/"low"
- `bd create ... --parent=<id>` - Hierarchical child (task under epic, subtask under task; inherits parent labels)
- `bd update <id> --claim` - Claim work
- `bd unclaim <id>` - Release stuck issue (agent crashed)
- `bd update <id> --assignee=username` - Assign to someone
- `bd update <id> --if-assignee=<expected> --assignee=<new>` - Atomic reassign: applies only if the assignee still matches (--if-status=<expected> guards status; --if-assignee='' requires unassigned). Mismatch exits non-zero with nothing written — never retry blindly
- `bd update <id> --title/--description/--notes/--design` - Update fields inline
- `bd close <id>` - Mark complete
- `bd close <id1> <id2> ...` - Close multiple issues at once (more efficient)
- `bd close <id> --reason="explanation"` - Close with reason
- **Tip**: When creating multiple issues/tasks/epics, use parallel subagents for efficiency
- **WARNING**: Do NOT use `bd edit` - it opens $EDITOR (vim/nano) which blocks agents

### Dependencies & Blocking
- `bd dep add <issue> <depends-on>` - Add dependency (issue depends on depends-on)
- `bd blocked` - Show all blocked issues
- `bd show <id>` - See what's blocking/blocked by this issue

### Sync & Collaboration
- `bd search <query>` - Search issues by keyword

### Project Health
- `bd stats` - Project statistics (open/closed/blocked counts)
- `bd doctor` - Check for issues (sync problems, missing hooks)
- `bd doctor --check=conventions` - Check for convention drift (lint, stale, orphans)

### Quality Tools
- `bd create --validate` - Check description has required sections
- `bd create --acceptance="criteria"` - Set acceptance criteria (checked by --validate)
- `bd create --design="decisions"` - Record design decisions
- `bd create --notes="context"` - Add supplementary notes
- `bd config set validation.on-create warn` - Auto-validate on every create
- `bd lint` - Check existing issues for missing sections

### Lifecycle & Hygiene
- `bd defer <id> --until="date"` - Defer work to a future date
- `bd supersede <id> --with=<new-id>` - Mark issue as superseded
- `bd close <id> --suggest-next` - Show newly unblocked issues after closing
- `bd stale` - Find issues with no recent activity
- `bd orphans` - Find issues with broken dependencies
- `bd preflight` - Pre-PR checks (lint, stale, orphans)
- `bd human <id>` - Flag for human decision (list/respond/dismiss)

### Structured Workflows
- `bd formula list` - See available workflow templates
- `bd mol pour <name>` - Start structured workflow from formula

**Starting work:**
```bash
bd ready           # Find available work

```bash
bd close <id1> <id2> ...    # Close all completed issues at once

# Run bd create commands in parallel (use subagents for many items)

bd create --title="Write tests for X" --description="Why this issue exists and what needs to be done" --type=task
bd dep add beads-yyy beads-xxx  # Tests depend on Feature (Feature blocks tests)
```

## 5. The device analysis (one fixpoint)

The device analysis is a whole-program, flow-insensitive, inclusion-based (Andersen-style),
field-sensitive points-to and value-set analysis over every device variant and every host
function that exchanges data with one (passes it a pointer, or shares a global it accesses). It
is specified exactly: **every verdict, candidate set and dispatch domain of this document is
computed from the reference analysis below.** A plugin MAY use a more precise analysis to shrink
dispatch domains or arena placement in its lowering, never to change a verdict (the run-time
value is always a member of the reference set, so the results are unchanged).

### 5.1 Abstract objects and atoms

The **abstract objects** are:

- each `alloca` instruction;
- each global variable;
- each **allocation site**: a call of a function that carries `allockind("alloc")` or
  `allocsize(...)`, or whose name is one of `malloc`, `calloc`, `realloc`, `aligned_alloc`,
  `posix_memalign` (the object is the one written through its first argument), `memalign`,
  `strdup`, `strndup`, `_Znwm`, `_Znam`, `_ZnwmRKSt9nothrow_t`, `_ZnamRKSt9nothrow_t`,
  `_ZnwmSt11align_val_t`, `_ZnamSt11align_val_t`, `__rust_alloc`, `__rust_alloc_zeroed`,
  `__rust_realloc`, `julia.gc_alloc_obj`, `ijl_gc_alloc_typed`, `jl_gc_alloc`,
  `ijl_gc_small_alloc`, `ijl_gc_big_alloc`, or `__bennett_nz_alloc`;
- the pseudo-object `EXT` (memory of unknown provenance: host memory reached through a pointer
  the analysis did not create).

Heap objects are context-insensitive: one object per allocation-site instruction of the variant
in which it occurs. The list above is the reference list; `bennett-runtimes` declares its
allocator entry points with `allockind("alloc")` so that they are covered by the first rule.

An **offset** is an integer byte offset, a **residue class** (r mod s)[lo, hi) with s ≥ 2 (every
offset o with o ≡ r (mod s) and lo ≤ o < hi), or `*` (any offset). An **atom** is one of:

- `(X, o)`: a pointer into object X at offset o;
- `FN(F)`: the address A(F) of a defined function F;
- `ROOT(r)`: a handle with root r (the allocation site of a `qalloc`/`qborrow` call, §6.5);
- `INT(c)`: the integer constant c (of the value's width).

Every SSA value v (per lane for vectors, per index path for aggregates) has an **abstract
value** AV(v) = (A, t): a finite set A of atoms and a flag t; t = ⊤ means "and any other integer
or address". ⊤ never stands for a `ROOT` atom (handle provenance is always explicit); for a
function-pointer operand it stands for "any function of the operand's type whose address is
used" (§7.2, §14.2). Every slot (X, o) of memory has a content AV(X, o) of the same form. When A
holds more than **K = 4096** `INT` atoms they are dropped and t is set (a normative constant: it
decides dispatch and blocker verdicts, so §24 does not defer it). "AV(v) = ⊤" below means t is
set; unions and inclusions are taken component-wise.

Abstract-object and return propagation MUST cover attributed allocator calls and defined allocator wrappers through their underlying allocation sites without requiring the wrapper's name in a whitelist. [deps: DLT-5.1-P4, DLT-5.1-P6, DLT-5.2-P10, ISA-7.1, ISA-7.2]

### 5.2 Rules

Before the fixpoint, seed the initialiser atoms of every global, mutable or constant,
into its slots. For each alloca and allocation-site object, also represent every extent
whose initial contents are zero under §§17.1/17.3 as a zero-byte initial image; represent a
nonconstant extent symbolically over its possible allocated bytes. Subsequent stores union
additional possibilities and never erase these initial possibilities. This is an initial
image, not a claim that the object remains immutable.
The analysis is the least fixpoint of the following rules over AV. "Copies" means AV(result) ⊇
AV(operand) lane by lane; ⊤ absorbs. S2 models the value effects of the specified
normalisation before verdicts and dispatch domains freeze; pre-normalisation syntax does
not permit omitting a value produced by the materialised execution.

| Rule | Instruction or source | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| PT1 | `alloca`, global `@G`, allocation-site call | AV ∋ (X, 0); zero-filled initial extents are seeded as above |
| PT2 | `getelementptr T, ptr p, idx…` | uses P(a), I(a) and the complete finite-width computation of §16.4, also for constant expressions: sequential indices are sign-extended/truncated to I(a), scaling and offset accumulation wrap modulo 2^I(a), and addition preserves pointer bits above I(a). Zero accumulated offset copies AV(p). For every (X,o), include the image of all represented base addresses and index values (unknown base bits range over patterns consistent with DataLayout and X's alignment), using integer/residue-class/`*` offset unions. Unbounded mathematical addition is not a substitute. If an address may leave X, include (EXT, `*`) and the non-handle top flag; PT6 propagates this unknown-address alternative. The unknown-address alternative does not by itself establish host escape (§6.4): retain the original base provenance for that test; actual host/unknown origins keep their restrictions. ROOT provenance and §6.2 restrictions remain explicit. Array dimensions determine stride, never an index bound |
| PT3 | `phi`, `select` (value operands), `freeze`, `addrspacecast`; `bitcast` | the first four copy (union). A bitcast copies lane by lane only when source and destination have identical lane counts and per-lane representation widths, treating a scalar as one lane. Otherwise use the virtual N21 bitcast expansion of §12.6: pack source-lane bit patterns into its prescribed bit string in DataLayout order, then resegment at the destination representation width. Compute output AV through PT4/PT5 transfers of that expansion; finite INT lane sets denote their full Cartesian product, and K applies per resulting lane. Never copy a source-lane INT atom as the value of a packed destination scalar. Unknown bits propagate non-handle top through those transfers. Explicit object and ROOT provenance follows the same expansion and remains subject to §6.2; top never substitutes for ROOT. Apply also to constant expressions and fixed-vscale forms during S2 before verdicts, candidate sets, shapes and dispatch domains freeze. Direct finite bit-string evaluation is equivalent; no LLVM optimisation is required |
| PT4 | `ptrtoint`, `inttoptr`, `zext`/`sext`/`trunc` | copy only bit-preserving conversions. Otherwise apply the actual source/destination-width bit-vector conversion (§16.4): ptrtoint/inttoptr zero-extend or truncate at P(a), sext sign-extends. Use PT2’s represented-address image construction, retaining originating object and ROOT provenance. A narrowed representation that cannot be reassociated with its original object adds (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤; numerical uncertainty is not host escape. Apply also to constant expressions |
| PT5 | `add`/`sub` of a value with pointer atoms and an `INT` set | computes address bits modulo 2^W at the integer operation’s width W, never unbounded offset addition. Relative displacements from the originating base are modular too: zero net displacement represents that base again, including after an inttoptr truncation to P(a). Use PT2’s represented-address image construction at W bits (full-width arithmetic, no preserved high GEP bits), retaining original object and ROOT provenance. Keep modular displacement alternatives even outside the allocation, and additionally include (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤ when an image may leave it. Residue classes and `*` take the same finite-width image; an unrepresentable image retains (X, `*`) for each originating X plus (EXT, `*`) and non-handle ⊤. Other integer operations on pointer atoms use that conservative image. Widening a pointer-derived integer by zext/sext uses that conservative image too (fixed reference fallback); ptrtoint zero-extension retains its base representation. Numerical uncertainty does not itself establish host escape (PT2). The rules apply also to constant expressions, before candidate/dispatch domains freeze; on finite `INT` sets, every LLVM integer operation, cast and `icmp` is evaluated elementwise; with x ⊤ and a constant m, `and x, m` gives the submasks of m and `urem x, m` gives {0, …, m−1} (each still subject to K); a range attribute or `!range` metadata removed by N3/N4 is not a bound on materialised execution and MUST NOT restrict any verdict, candidate set, descriptor shape or dispatch domain. Only bounds independently established by materialised instructions restrict the set; otherwise retain ⊤ and apply the ordinary operation rules, including finite masks/remainders |
| PT6 | `load T, ptr p` | for each (X,o) ∈ AV(p): memory atoms carry their storage interval and representation width; copy a whole-value atom only for that identical interval with a compatible representation. A known immutable byte image decodes exactly the accessed bytes using DataLayout and T (including initialisers). For a partial, spanning or overlapping access whose result bits are not represented exactly, the non-handle value is ⊤, never the union of component INT values. X=EXT gives ⊤. A non-handle top flag on p contributes the same unknown-address load alternative even when explicit object offsets overlap no known slot; it MUST NOT be dropped. ROOT provenance remains explicit and §6.2's partial-handle restrictions still apply. For a possibly valid load wholly contained in a zero-filled initial extent, include the all-zero value of the load’s representation and width, unioned with values from every overlapping store or copy under these interval rules. PT8 propagates these initial byte possibilities when copying memory. Zero contributes no ROOT. Region inputs, replayed caller bytes and other nonzero-initialised contents retain their specified sources; this rule does not replace them with zeros |
| PT7 | `store v, ptr p` | retain each written interval and representation width; partial writes overlapping a larger load participate in PT6's conservative fallback. AV(X, o + d_k) ⊇ AV(v_k) for each (X, o) ∈ AV(p) and each lane or field k at byte offset d_k |
| PT8 | memory copy (`llvm.memcpy`/`memmove`/`memcpy.inline`, `__bennett_rt_memmove`) of (dst, src, len); memory fill (`llvm.memset`, `__bennett_rt_memset`) of (dst, v, len) | copy: propagate zero-byte initial images over the copied byte ranges, including their symbolic extents; for each slot (Y, s) of each (Y, o_s) ∈ AV(src) and (Z, o_d) ∈ AV(dst): when offsets and len are constants and the atom's entire storage interval [s, s + size(atom)) lies in the copied range, copy its atom and representation width to (Z, o_d + s − o_s). Every partially overlapping atom instead contributes non-handle ⊤ and explicit ROOT provenance to the affected destination interval (unknown offsets/len use `*`); do not copy the whole component INT. Fill: every slot in [o_d, o_d + len) of each (Z, o_d) ∈ AV(dst) (all of Z when an offset or len is not constant) gets the `INT` atoms of the byte v repeated to the slot's width (⊤ when v is not constant) |
| PT9 | direct call of G, or indirect call with candidate G (§14.2) | AV(parameter i of G) ⊇ AV(argument i); AV(result) ⊇ the union of AV over G's `ret` operands; `byval` arguments copy the pointee's slots into the callee's copy object |
| PT10 | `insertelement`/`extractelement`/`shufflevector`/`insertvalue`/`extractvalue` | extraction/insertion use N15’s total unsigned index semantics. Constant in-range extraction copies its lane; constant out-of-range extraction gives the element type’s zero. Dynamic extraction unions every source lane selected by a represented index, plus zero iff the index set includes an unsigned value >= L (including top when 2^K > L). No zero is added if every represented index is in range; zero adds no ROOT. In-range insertion replaces the selected lane; out-of-range insertion preserves the vector. Dynamic insertion includes each old lane unless it is certainly replaced, and the inserted value whenever that lane can be selected. Valid shuffle indices and aggregate paths move atoms unchanged (handle-table reads, audit B-23) |
| PT11 | a parameter of a function with a host caller, a device entry, or a function whose address host code uses | AV ⊇ {(EXT, `*`)} for `ptr` parameters, ⊤ for integer parameters, plus the atoms of the device-side arguments by PT9; for a pointer parameter i of a device entry E, also the candidate atom ROOT(E, i) of §6.6 |
| PT12 | a `qalloc`/`qborrow` call; a call of a function with no definition that resolves to a host call (§4.5) or is trapped; an `atomicrmw`/`cmpxchg` (before N9) | `qalloc`/`qborrow`: AV ∋ ROOT(site) (§6.5). An undefined callee: the result is ⊤ and every slot reachable from its pointer arguments gets ⊤. Before domains freeze, PT12 MUST apply PT6 to the old value, the PT5 transfers of the full N9 update, and PT7 to the actual stored value, with cmpxchg returning its old-value and success fields and storing the selected new-or-old value. [deps: DLT-5.2-P1, DLT-5.2-P6, DLT-5.2-P7, DLT-5.2-P8, DLT-5.2-P13, DLT-12.2-P11, ISA-4.1, ISA-4.2] |

An `inttoptr` of a constant gives (EXT, `*`) and, for function-pointer purposes, ⊤: function
addresses are symbolic `FN` atoms during the analysis, and their numeric values A(F) are fixed
only by the comparison link (§19.5), after normalisation (S7) and before lifting (S8).


**PT12 N9 update witnesses (DB/DN/DS; non-normative).** The global starts at 1. Xor by 2 alternates 1 and 3 in the flow-insensitive least fixpoint; a single execution allocates width 3. Cmpxchg against 1 first succeeds and writes 3; subsequent abstract iterations include failure on old 3. Against 9 it always fails and leaves 1. Native LLVM 18/23 materialised O0/O3 checks old/success, actual width, clean free, liveness and code 0.
  call void @__bennett_qfree(ptr %b)
  ret void
}
```

```text state=compatible-allocator-graph at=return
{"roots":{"%from_node":["top%a"],"%from_callback":["top%a","top%b"],"%lane":["top%a","top%b"]},"counter_roots":[],"native":[[9,2,4,2,0,0],[9,2,4,2,0,0]]}
```

### 5.3 Value sets

The **value set** VS(v) of an integer or pointer operand is the set of its `INT` and `FN` atoms
(pointer atoms denote addresses that are not compile-time constants and make VS(v) = ⊤ for
dispatch purposes), or ⊤. A value set is a precision tool only:

- an operand with a finite VS is dispatched over it (§3.4, §14.2);
- an operand with VS = ⊤ is dispatched over its type's whole valid domain where that domain is
  finite: operation codes and classes (§3.4), binding kinds (§8.4), the function addresses of a
  type (§7.2, §14.2);
- otherwise it is a blocker case of §25.1: widths (BLK-1), budgets computed on the device
  (BLK-2), parameters of in-place pairs (§8.7).

### 5.4 Uses

| Result | Read by |
|---|---|
| `FN` atoms of region operands | region candidate sets (§7.2) |
| `FN` atoms of indirect callees | indirect-call candidate sets (§14.2) |
| `ROOT` atoms | handle provenance (§6.2), handle containment (§6.4), static register files (§6.5) |
| `INT` atoms of descriptor and budget fields | descriptor shapes (§8.4), budget pre-minting (§9.5) |
| slots stored under control | the may-Co analysis and arena placement (§10, TDL-2, TDL-4) |
| objects loaded by L code | the oracle read set R and entry-state transfer (§8.5) |
| `INT` atoms of `op`, `cls`, `n` | dispatch domains (§3.4) |

```llvm example=pt-variable-index verdict=accept
%struct.tab = type { [4 x ptr], i64 }

define void @t(i64 %i) {
  %s = alloca %struct.tab
  %h0 = call ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 0, i64 1)
  store ptr %h0, ptr %s
  %pn = getelementptr %struct.tab, ptr %s, i64 0, i32 1
  store i64 3, ptr %pn
  %k = and i64 %i, 3
  %pk = getelementptr %struct.tab, ptr %s, i64 0, i32 0, i64 %k
  %hk = load ptr, ptr %pk
  call void @__bennett_gate(i32 1, ptr %hk, i64 0)
  %n = load i64, ptr %pn
  %n2 = add i64 %n, 1
  store i64 %n2, ptr %pn
  call void @__bennett_qfree(ptr %h0)
  ret void
}
```

*(non-normative)* In `pt-variable-index`, `%pk` has the offset class (0 mod 8)[0, 32) in `%s`
(the `[4 x ptr]` array), so the load `%hk` reads the slots 0, 8, 16, 24: AV(`%hk`) =
{ROOT(`%h0`)}. The count field is at the exact offset 32, outside the class, so AV(`%n`) holds
INT(3) and, through the store of `%n2`, INT(4), INT(5), … until it exceeds K and becomes ⊤; it
holds no `ROOT` atom, and the arithmetic on it is legal (audit B-27: 0.1 gave the load offset `*`
and rejected this program).

**Finite-width and materialised-domain witnesses (round 6).** Under I=32,
2^32 truncates to zero, so the table load yields FN(body) and dispatch succeeds.
For the ranged inputs, N3/N4 erase the annotations: `n & 3` gives {0,1,2,3}
and `| 1` gives {1,3}. At n=2 width 3 admits both X gates on wire 2 and a clean
free. No new input precondition or fault is introduced.

## 6. Handles (handles 0.2.8)

### 6.1 Representation

A handle is an ordinary `ptr` in address space 0. In device memory and in integers a handle is
its **register tag**: an opaque value below 2^pw assigned to an allocation generation
(§6.5), nonzero and distinct from every identity still observable through live or stale
copies. Physical slots are reusable storage, never allocation identity. A tag MUST NOT
be recycled while an observable stale copy can remain; integer wrap is not permission
to alias generations. Copies continue to denote their original generation after free. ISA 0.8 has no instruction that
reads a `qreg`'s launch id (BLK-1's `qreg.id` would add one), so tags are the plugin's. At the
host–device boundary a handle is the ISA launch register id (`bennett_qreg`, ISA §10), which the
launch binds to a `qreg` parameter or result (§6.6). Neither value may influence a result other
than through §6.3; the rules below make them unobservable. A handle
denotes a register of n wires, n the `qalloc` operand; wire i of handle h is written h.i.

### 6.2 Provenance (maintainer decision 2)

The **provenance** of a value is the set of `ROOT` atoms of its abstract value (§5). Provenance
flows through **bitwise copies** only: `ptrtoint`/`inttoptr` at width pw, integer loads and
stores of width pw, vector lanes (including dynamic lanes, PT10: a dynamically indexed vector of
handles is a handle table), aggregate fields, memory copies, `phi`, `select`, `freeze`, call
arguments and returns (PT3–PT10). A handle-derived value (any lane or field) MAY be:

1. a handle operand of a marker (§3.2);
2. copied by the rules above, including storage in any slot the analysis tracks (§6.4);
3. an operand of `icmp eq` or `icmp ne` (§6.3);
4. an argument of a direct or indirect call of a defined function, or a return value.

Every other use is `BD-E-HANDLE-USE` (maintainer decision 2: "any other use of a
handle-derived integer is rejected fail-loud"; NS §3.1 "never dereferenced"): in particular
arithmetic, bitwise operations, `icmp` with any other predicate, any cast other than the copies
above (`trunc`, `zext`, `sext`, `bitcast` to a non-pointer-width type, `ptrtoint` to a width ≠
pw), `getelementptr` with a handle as base or index, `switch` on it, use as the pointer operand of
a `load`, `store`, `atomicrmw`, `cmpxchg` or memory intrinsic (dereference), and a load of any
type whose byte range overlaps a handle-derived slot without being exactly one pw-wide lane of
it (a partial read).

A handle operand of a marker whose provenance is empty is `BD-E-HANDLE-UNTRACKED` (it cannot be
a handle of this program). *(non-normative)* A tracked value that at run time is not a live
handle (a stale copy, a freed register) traps at the marker (`T-DEAD`, §18.3).

Dynamically indexed handle vectors MUST use PT10 lane provenance and N15 total index semantics, retaining explicit ROOT atoms and adding zero only for possible out-of-range extraction. [deps: DLT-5.2-P11, DLT-6.2-P1, DLT-12.2-P17, ISA-4.1, ISA-4.2]

### 6.3 Comparison (maintainer decision 4)

`icmp eq` and `icmp ne` of a handle with `null`, or with another handle-derived value, compare
**identity**: a live handle is never null, and two handles are equal iff they denote the same
allocation generation, including when comparing a freed identity with a later allocation
in the same physical slot. The result is not handle-derived. In CC it lowers to `icmp` on register tags. This
covers C `if (!q)` and Rust's `Option<Handle>` niche (audit D8). *(non-normative)* A comparison
is a classical observation of identity, not of the register's content; ids are unique, so it
equals the native comparison of the two handle values.

Handle equality and null tests MUST compare allocation identity, preserve stale-versus-new inequality and produce a result without handle provenance. [deps: DLT-6.1-P1, DLT-6.3-P1, ISA-3.3-P7, ISA-4.1-P4]

### 6.4 Collections and containment

Handles may be stored in any slot the analysis tracks: tables, vectors, linked structures,
globals (field-sensitively), heap objects, and objects passed to indirect calls or returned by
address-taken functions (audit B-22, B-24, B-26, B-29). A handle may **escape** only where code
the analysis cannot see could read it:

- `BD-E-HANDLE-ESCAPE`: a `ROOT` atom reaches a slot of `EXT`, an argument or a reachable slot of
  a call that resolves to a host call or is trapped (§4.5), or the return value of a function
  whose callers are not all in the link, other than a device entry's `QREG` result (§6.6). NS §3.1: "any handle use that survives into host native
  code is rejected".

H functions in the link that receive launch register ids through §6.6 are analysed like device code:
they may copy, store, compare and pass them back to device entries (§6.2 items 2–4); any other
use is `BD-E-HANDLE-USE`.

Handle provenance MUST flow through every indirect-call candidate's parameters and results exactly as through a direct defined call. [deps: DLT-5.2-P10, DLT-6.2-P5, DLT-6.4, ISA-3.3-P21, ISA-4.1-P11]

A tracked object containing handles MUST remain storable and returnable through other tracked objects, with escape decided by the reachable handle slots rather than object escape alone. [deps: DLT-5.2, DLT-6.4-P1, DLT-6.4-P2, ISA-7.1, ISA-3.3]

A global handle field MUST be analysed by reachable byte slots, allowing analysed host transport under the handle rules and retaining rejection only for an actual prohibited use or unknown-reader escape. [deps: DLT-5.2, DLT-6.4, DLT-6.6, ISA-3.3, ISA-10]

### 6.5 Realisation on ISA 0.8: static register files

ISA 0.8 has no instruction that stores, loads or converts a `qreg` (ISA §3.2, §3.3, §4.1), and
`qreg[n]` types have literal widths. CC code holds `qreg` values in its classical environment
(dynamic SSA instances, block parameters, call arguments and results; ISA §3.2, §7.4). The plugin
realises each allocation site s (a `qalloc` or `qborrow` call) in one of two ways:

- **Direct.** If no `ROOT(s)` atom reaches a memory slot (PT7, PT8), the handle is a CC `qreg`
  value threaded through SSA values, block parameters, call arguments and results ("register-
  environment passing"; §25.2 records that ISA §3.3's transition table should name CC `call`,
  `ret` and `br` operands of `qreg` type). Every execution of s creates a new dynamic instance,
  so loops and recursion need no bound.
- **Static register file.** Otherwise a physical **slot** is a pair (s, instance
  index k < N(s)); ROOT(s) is static provenance, not run-time identity. For
  each slot the plugin keeps one CC `qreg` variable, threaded like a direct handle through every
  CC variant that may use it; `qalloc` at s picks a free instance index at run time and executes
  `qreg.new cls n` into that variable (one variable per member of the width dispatch domain,
  §3.4). The default CC table holds each slot's current generation tag and liveness;
  a fresh-tag allocator gives every allocation a new identity (§6.1), stored in memory.
  A marker operand is dispatched by matching its full tag against current live candidate
  slots (compare tree, §14.2); a missing or retired generation traps T-DEAD before
  changing any register. Free retires the identity even if the slot is immediately reused.
  Recycle tags only after old copies are proven unobservable, never by slot reuse alone.
  This table and allocator use ordinary classical operations, without qreg.id.
  N(s) bounds simultaneous physical slots, not the number of historical identities. A direct handle that is converted to an integer
  (`ptrtoint`, a store) gets a tag in the same way.
